Yeah… it’s changed, I think. I mean, I still want to be the kind of person who enjoys those things, if that makes sense. Probably… a little of both, but more that I just don’t feel the pull to do them anymore. I used to make time for hiking and yoga, and I liked being in my garden or taking bird photos. Lately I keep canceling or putting it off, and my camera just sits in the closet.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thanks for seeing me. I guess I’m here because I’ve just been really tired—probably more than I should be. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing I should come in for, but it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but yes, I think so. Most days I wake up with this really heavy, flat feeling in my chest. Is that what you mean by feeling down?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed, I think. I mean, I still want to be the kind of person who enjoys those things, if that makes sense. I used to make time for hiking and yoga, and I really loved working in my garden or taking bird photos. Lately I’ll plan to go, or I’ll tell myself I should, and then I just… cancel, or don’t follow through. My camera’s basically been sitting in the closet.\n\nIt’s not that I hate those things now. It’s more like none of it feels worth the effort, which sounds bad when I say it out loud. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I’d say I want to die, exactly. I don’t want to worry anyone or make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut… late at night, sometimes I do catch myself thinking that maybe my husband would be better off without me. And I haven’t made any kind of plan or anything like that, but the thought keeps coming back enough that it scares me a little. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… if I’m being honest, it’s been most days for about two months now. The heavy, slowed-down, exhausted feeling. I kept thinking it would pass or that I was just having a rough week, so I didn’t really want to make too much of it, but… yeah, around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. Maybe a little? I wouldn’t say I’m constantly anxious or anything like that, at least I don’t think so.\n\nI do feel kind of tense sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by “on edge.” I don’t want to overstate it. Like, compared to everything else, it doesn’t feel like the main thing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I haven’t really noticed anything significant, no. My clothes seem to fit about the same, I think. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly or anything, so I could be missing something, but nothing that’s really stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned earlier that you haven't been following through with hiking, yoga, and other activities you used to enjoy. I used to make time for hiking and yoga, and I liked being in my garden or taking bird photos. Lately I keep canceling or putting it off, and my camera just sits in the closet.\n\nIt’s not that I hate those things now or anything… it just feels like too much effort, and then I think, “What’s the point?” I don’t know if that counts as losing interest, but it kind of feels that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’ve been restless, really. And if he asks me something simple, sometimes it takes me a while to answer. Like the words are there, but they come out really slowly.\n\nI didn’t really notice it as much myself until he pointed it out. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a bit. Not really fidgety, no… I don’t think I’ve had trouble sitting still. If anything, it’s more the opposite, like I said.

The heavy, slowed-down, exhausted feeling. I kept thinking it would pass or that I was just having a rough week, so I didn’t really want to make too much of it, but… yeah, around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. Maybe a little? I wouldn’t say I’m constantly anxious or anything like that, at least I don’t think so.\n\nI do feel kind of tense sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by “on edge.” I don’t want to overstate it. Like, compared to everything else, it doesn’t feel like the main thing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I haven’t really noticed anything significant, no. Maybe a little? I wouldn’t say I’m constantly anxious or anything like that, at least I don’t think so.\n\nI do feel kind of tense sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by “on edge.” I don’t want to overstate it. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing I should come in for, but it’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but yes, I think so. Most days I wake up with this really heavy, flat feeling in my chest. I mean, I still want to be the kind of person who enjoys those things, if that makes sense. I used to make time for hiking and yoga, and I really loved working in my garden or taking bird photos. Maybe a little? I wouldn’t say I’m constantly anxious or anything like that, at least I don’t think so.\n\nI do feel kind of tense sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by “on edge.” I don’t want to overstate it. Like, compared to everything else, it doesn’t feel like the main thing. Is that because you've lost interest in them or is it just that they feel like too much effort?\n\nPatient: Probably… a little of both, but more that I just don’t feel the pull to do them anymore. I used to make time for hiking and yoga, and I liked being in my garden or taking bird photos. Lately I keep canceling or putting it off, and my camera just sits in the closet.\n\nIt’s not that I hate those things now or anything… it just feels like too much effort, and then I think, “What’s the point?” I don’t know if that counts as losing interest, but it kind of feels that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’ve been restless, really. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a bit. I know that may not be completely fair, but it just keeps coming up in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling fidgety or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, no… I don’t think I’ve had trouble sitting still. If anything, it’s more the opposite, like I said.\n\nMy husband has noticed I’m moving really slowly around the house—he says it’s like I’m underwater. And sometimes when he asks me something simple, I take longer than I should to answer. It’s like I know what I want to say, but the words come out slowly. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “worthless” exactly… that sounds a little strong, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut I do keep thinking I was selfish or reckless with the mortgage decision. Like I somehow put our whole household at risk, even though my husband keeps reminding me we made the decision together. I know that logically, but I still keep replaying it and feeling like… maybe I’m the reason things could go wrong. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of sadness and hopelessness nearly every day for the past two months?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, most days. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I am getting through the day, but a lot of mornings I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling in my chest.\n\nSometimes I’ll just sit at the kitchen table and stare out at the yard, and it feels like there isn’t really a good version of the future coming. That sounds bleak when I say it out loud, I know… but that’s honestly how it’s been feeling, pretty regularly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've lost pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, or is it more that they just feel like too much effort to do?\n\nPatient: I think it’s probably both, if that makes sense. My camera’s been sitting in the closet, and even the garden feels like this big task instead of something I enjoy. It’s like I tell myself I’ll feel better if I do it, but then none of it feels worth the effort. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are mildly uncomfortable, moderately distressing, or severely debilitating for you?\n\nPatient: I’d say… moderately distressing, probably. Most days I wake up with that heavy, flat feeling in my chest, and I can end up just sitting at the kitchen table staring out at the yard, thinking there isn’t really a good version of the future coming. Sometimes I’ll just sit at the kitchen table looking out at the yard, and it’s like… I can’t imagine anything ahead feeling better. Like there isn’t a good version of the future coming. Is that what you mean by feeling down?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed, I think. I mean, I still want to be the kind of person who enjoys those things, if that makes sense. I used to make time for hiking and yoga, and I really loved working in my garden or taking bird photos. Lately I’ll plan to go, or I’ll tell myself I should, and then I just… cancel, or don’t follow through. My camera’s basically been sitting in the closet.\n\nIt’s not that I hate those things now. It’s more like none of it feels worth the effort, which sounds bad when I say it out loud. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I’d say I want to die, exactly. I don’t want to worry anyone or make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut… late at night, sometimes I do catch myself thinking that maybe my husband would be better off without me. And I haven’t made any kind of plan or anything like that, but the thought keeps coming back enough that it scares me a little. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… if I’m being honest, it’s been most days for about two months now. The heavy, slowed-down, exhausted feeling. I kept thinking it would pass or that I was just having a rough week, so I didn’t really want to make too much of it, but… yeah, around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. Maybe a little? I wouldn’t say I’m constantly anxious or anything like that, at least I don’t think so.\n\nI do feel kind of tense sometimes, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by “on edge.” I don’t want to overstate it. Like, compared to everything else, it doesn’t feel like the main thing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I haven’t really noticed anything significant, no. My clothes seem to fit about the same, I think. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly or anything, so I could be missing something, but nothing that’s really stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned earlier that you haven't been following through with hiking, yoga, and other activities you used to enjoy. Is that because you've lost interest in them or is it just that they feel like too much effort?\n\nPatient: Probably… a little of both, but more that I just don’t feel the pull to do them anymore. I used to make time for hiking and yoga, and I liked being in my garden or taking bird photos. Lately I keep canceling or putting it off, and my camera just sits in the closet.\n\nIt’s not that I hate those things now or anything… it just feels like too much effort, and then I think, “What’s the point?” I don’t know if that counts as losing interest, but it kind of feels that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’ve been restless, really. If anything, it’s more the slowed-down thing, I guess.\n\nMy husband has mentioned it a couple times… he says I’m moving around the house like I’m underwater, which sounds dramatic, but I kind of know what he means. And if he asks me something simple, sometimes it takes me a while to answer. Like the words are there, but they come out really slowly.\n\nI didn’t really notice it as much myself until he pointed it out. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m blaming everyone or being dramatic, but I keep going back over this mortgage decision we made.\n\nEven though my husband tells me we decided it together, I keep thinking I was selfish, or reckless… like I somehow pushed us into something that put the whole household at risk. I know that may not be completely fair, but it just keeps coming up in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling fidgety or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, no… I don’t think I’ve had trouble sitting still. If anything, it’s more the opposite, like I said.\n\nMy husband has noticed I’m moving really slowly around the house—he says it’s like I’m underwater. And sometimes when he asks me something simple, I take longer than I should to answer. It’s like I know what I want to say, but the words come out slowly. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “worthless” exactly… that sounds a little strong, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut I do keep thinking I was selfish or reckless with the mortgage decision. Like I somehow put our whole household at risk, even though my husband keeps reminding me we made the decision together. I know that logically, but I still keep replaying it and feeling like… maybe I’m the reason things could go wrong. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of sadness and hopelessness nearly every day for the past two months?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, most days. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I am getting through the day, but a lot of mornings I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling in my chest.\n\nSometimes I’ll just sit at the kitchen table and stare out at the yard, and it feels like there isn’t really a good version of the future coming. That sounds bleak when I say it out loud, I know… but that’s honestly how it’s been feeling, pretty regularly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've lost pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, or is it more that they just feel like too much effort to do?\n\nPatient: I think it’s probably both, if that makes sense. I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve stopped everything completely, but… I used to be pretty good about making time for hiking, yoga, working in the garden, taking bird photos.\n\nLately I keep canceling plans or just not going. My camera’s been sitting in the closet, and even the garden feels like this big task instead of something I enjoy. It’s like I tell myself I’ll feel better if I do it, but then none of it feels worth the effort. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are mildly uncomfortable, moderately distressing, or severely debilitating for you?\n\nPatient: I’d say… moderately distressing, probably. I don’t think I’m completely unable to function, so “severely debilitating” feels like too much.\n\nBut it’s more than mildly uncomfortable. Most days I wake up with that heavy, flat feeling in my chest, and I can end up just sitting at the kitchen table staring out at the yard, thinking there isn’t really a good version of the future coming. So… moderate sounds right, if that’s the way you’d put it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty concentrating or making decisions on a daily basis?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I’m still doing my job, so I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s been happening pretty much daily.\n\nAt work I’ll read the same survey results or a client email two or three times and still feel like it didn’t really go in. And sometimes even deciding something simple, like which spreadsheet to open first, feels weirdly impossible for a few minutes. I know that sounds small, but it’s not really normal for me. Is that the kind of concentration problem you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are interfering with your daily life most of the time or nearly all of the time?\n\nPatient: I think… most of the time, yes. I wouldn’t say nearly all the time, because I am still getting up and going to work and doing the basic things at home.\n\nBut it is interfering. I’ve fallen behind on some client reports, which I’m embarrassed to admit, and I’ve been avoiding friends more than I should. I’m also not really keeping up with the outdoor routines that usually help keep me steady. It feels like most of my energy just goes into getting through work and the basic stuff at home, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of sadness and hopelessness are affecting your ability to work or take care of things at home to a significant degree?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, though I feel a little bad saying it that way because I’m still functioning. I’m still showing up to work and keeping the house basically okay.\n\nBut I have fallen behind on some client reports, and at home it feels like I’m mostly just doing the minimum. I’ve also been avoiding friends and not keeping up with the outdoor routines that usually help me feel steady. So… yes, I guess it’s affecting things to a significant degree, just not completely stopping me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
There are parts of the day where I can be a little better, yes. I don’t want to make it sound like it’s constant every single minute.

But most days, especially in the morning, I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling in my chest. I’ll sit at the kitchen table and look out at the yard, and it’s like… I can’t picture a good version of the future. That sounds dramatic when I say it, but that’s honestly what it feels like. Then sometimes later I can get moving a bit, but that feeling is there most days.

Like, compared to everything else, it doesn’t feel like the main thing. Like the words are there, but they come out really slowly.\n\nI didn’t really notice it as much myself until he pointed it out. I know that may not be completely fair, but it just keeps coming up in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling fidgety or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, no… I don’t think I’ve had trouble sitting still. I’m also not really keeping up with the outdoor routines that usually help keep me steady.
